### 11.13 Common per-run schema — top-level enforcement and identity (I01–I11, I21–I22)

Frozen Protocol v1.1 §9 (lines 1185–1207) defines 17 top-level paths for every
S1–S6 run record. `host/runner.py:1100–1110` has a shared presence list with 13
keys: the 12 listed below plus implementation field `execution_mode`. It only
adds `injection_hook`, `pre_state_dump`, and `post_state_dump` for S1. Current
source does not put `kill_condition_triggered` or `notes` in the common list.
The other three §9 paths are therefore not required by the shared path for
S2–S6; S6 uses separate `dump1/2/3_*` members. Missing-key source candidate.

| Frozen path / property | Current behavior and existing evidence | Remaining P/F/C/E control |
|---|---|---|
| `spike_id`, `case_id`, `injection_hook` | Presence is checked for `spike_id`/`case_id`; `spike_id` dispatches validators. S1 exact case↔hook is checked (`runner.py:1150–1154`), S2 case enum is closed (`1155–1158`), and S6 checks hook enum (`1797–1808`). S3 fixture and S5 scenario IDs are counted by their aggregate path; S5 per-run loop does not reject an unrecognized case ID, and no common hook/fixture registry is applied. Existing tests include `test_s2_invalid_case_id`, `test_reject_incompatible_case_id_and_injection_hook`, `test_s6_validator_rejects_unknown_hook_enum`; the S6 pytest-style test is not collected by check-all. | Enumerate every allowed spike/case/hook and conditional association from the frozen protocol; reject unknown/extra IDs and hook mismatch for S3–S5, with valid null/unused representation only when the protocol permits it. Preserve intentional S1/S6 hook semantics. |
| `run_id`, `operation_id` | `run_id` must be truthy and unique (`runner.py:1025–1035`); no common string/type/format check. `operation_id` key presence is required, but there is no common nonempty/type/uniqueness check. S1 uses it as the `.bin` filename (`1215–1217`); S2's operation-to-C1…C7 binding is not established by this field check. Existing coverage includes missing QUAL `run_id` and `test_reject_duplicate_run_id`. | Bind run and operation identity to their producer command/event and referenced artifacts; test null/blank, wrong JSON type, duplicate or foreign operation identity and mismatched filename/content. Do not invent an ID regex absent producer/frozen rule. |
| `device_build_fingerprint`, `app_build_hash`, `execution_mode` | Required by key presence; mode is checked against selected validator mode (`runner.py:1037–1043`). QUAL serial/DUT/model checks and fingerprint/hash tuple equality are conditional on truthy values (`1050–1093`), so empty strings bypass that equality branch. `execution_mode` is an implementation-required field absent from §9's 17-key example; the cycle manifest requires mode (`runner.py:948–958`) and Contract 05 requires aggregate mode (`docs/05_RESULTS_PACKAGE_CONTRACT.md:57–64`), but those do not explicitly specify a per-run mode member. | Require authoritative nonempty typed identity in QUAL and cross-bind manifest, per-run, dump, aggregate, installed APK/source and serial. Keep the existing per-run mode guard; map its provenance through actual producers and package contract before changing it. Do not treat it as a frozen §9 path. |
| `start_monotonic_ns`, `end_monotonic_ns` | Both must be positive strict integers, with end ≥ start (`runner.py:1112–1120`). Existing named controls include `test_reject_per_run_zero_start_monotonic_ns`; the broader timestamp test inventory does not yet establish both common fields across all spike types. | Add false/zero/fraction/string/reversed and cross-run epoch/order cases for both fields; confirm source clock and units without adding a performance threshold. |
| `measured_metrics` | Key presence is common; the common path does not require an object. S1/S2 and selected later spike branches read selected members, but no common shape exists. For S3, aggregate counts are not derived from this object in the inspected branch. | Type each metric object from its frozen per-spike map; missing/null/wrong-type value and aggregate/per-run disagreement mutations across S1–S6. |
| `artifact_checks` | Key presence is common; no shared list/element schema. S2 derives `check_names` from dict entries and requires two named checks (`runner.py:1163–1170`). Generic PASS contradiction code only inspects it when it is a list (`1369–1375`), so a non-list can bypass that generic scan. | Require the frozen list/object shape and closed check registry per spike; missing, duplicate, unknown, wrong-type and false checks must reconcile with aggregate outcome/exit status. Preserve the S1 intentional process-death result. |
| `raw_log_location` | Requires nonempty string, safe in-package path, existing nonempty regular file (`runner.py:1122–1129`). Existing tests cover traversal, missing file and null location. No generic binding of log contents to run/operation/hook/PID is performed by this check. | Reuse path-safety tests; add same-size foreign/stale log, wrong run/operation/hook/PID and overwritten-transcript evidence controls. Keep device transcript distinct from authoritative host result. |
| `pre_state_dump`, `post_state_dump` | Both are required nonempty objects only for S1 (`runner.py:1105–1106`, `1134–1143`); no explicit-null presence check for other spikes. S6's separate diagnostics do not enforce these §9 keys. | Require the two members for every run; use nonempty typed objects for applicable hooks and explicit schema-allowed null when inapplicable. Cross-bind applicable dumps to external files and run identity. |
| `kill_condition_triggered`, `notes` | Neither is in the shared required list. Generic aggregate-PASS contradiction handling accepts absence and only treats nonempty lists or selected strings (`"violation"`, `"fail"`, `"error"`) as a kill; booleans and other strings are not consistently typed (`runner.py:1362–1369`). S6 later requires kill hook equality; S1 intentionally triggers process death. The executed S4 DEV PASS fixture in `test_s4_aggregate_and_host_pdf_validation` omits both paths and passes in retained host log lines 283–288. | Define frozen per-spike null/value semantics, require presence and type, test false/true/list/string/null and unknown diagnostics, and reconcile each violation with run/aggregate/outcome. Do not classify expected S1/S6 injected deaths as product failures. `notes` remains nullable as the template shows; its type must be derived from producers. |

`test_s2_valid_dev_evidence` passes with a DEV `INCOMPLETE` aggregate while its
per-run record omits `injection_hook`, both state-dump paths,
`kill_condition_triggered`, and `notes` (retained host log line 276). The S4

by `tools/check_all.sh`; do not call that execution evidence.

**Consolidated regression contract candidate `COMMON-RUN-SCHEMA-01`:**

- Positive control: one valid per-run record for each S1–S6, with all 17 frozen
  members, correct conditional null/objects, identity bindings, and matching
  aggregate; include separate DEV and QUAL fixtures so DEV cannot satisfy QUAL.
- Negative set: remove each top-level member individually; substitute null,
  wrong JSON type, empty identity, unknown enum, foreign operation/log/dump,
  malformed `artifact_checks`, and contradictory kill/outcome values. For every
  applicable field, verify both per-run rejection and the package-level result.
- Reuse existing missing/duplicate run ID, timestamp, safe-log, dump-identity,
  S1 hook, S2 case/check and S6 enum controls; close the named untested cells
  rather than duplicating already-proven behavior.
- Trace the extra per-run `execution_mode` member to actual producer
  serialization and the package-level mode contract; retain the current guard
  while this mapping is open. Keep `outcome` classified as a runner extension
  because it is absent from §9's per-run template.

Candidate regression tests remain inside the existing consolidated I01–I25
batch; no code or version change is authorized until that inventory and batch

later operation, and unchanged earlier evidence where the contract requires it.

This is a static inventory/evidence reconciliation, not an implementation or
review verdict. No runner/test source or application version changed. I21/I22
are not yet fully covered; the remaining tests above stay in the single
consolidated I01–I25 batch.

**Cross-cutting preservation oracle:** every field mutation must begin from a
fully valid aggregate/per-run package for the same mode. A malformed independent
field must produce the contract's intended outcome/diagnostic, must not be
masked by an earlier unrelated failure, and must preserve raw source evidence.
Use strict JSON type distinctions (`bool` is not an integer count), closed
enums, and finite nonnegative values where the frozen field is a measurement;
do not add latency or accuracy thresholds. Separate `PASS` kill-counter
requirements from typed operation failures, missing required work, and
qualification-incomplete results.

**Resolved outcome interpretation `ENG-AGGREGATE-OUTCOME-01`:** Protocol §2.5
defines `FAIL` by the per-spike kill-condition lists and `INCOMPLETE` by missing
mandatory work/result/evidence. Therefore a typed operation failure or
implementation-only extension counter is not independently a kill; preserve it
and reconcile it to run evidence. A run with an identified typed failure can
coexist with aggregate `PASS` only when the mandatory work and evidence are
complete and no frozen kill is present. An unclassified/undetectable operation
result makes the aggregate `INCOMPLETE`. Apply the same rule to S1's
`failed_runs_count`/`oracle_violations*` extensions and the generic per-run
`outcome` / `artifact_checks` contradiction loop. S2 and S4 examples are explicit
in `execution/decisions.md`; this is a protocol-derived interpretation, not a
new outcome or threshold. Source deltas are concrete: S1's disk aggregator makes
any non-PASS run and any false artifact check a FAIL input; S2's producer also
treats typed failures, accepted/committed mismatch, failed health observation,
background error and unconfirmed permit overlap as kill inputs. S4 now records
per-run typed failures and validates committed outputs by explicit source status;
attempt 57 proves a complete typed-only failure can coexist with aggregate PASS.
Remaining spike-specific outcome cells stay in I12. In the regression contract,
only frozen kill conditions may yield FAIL; a typed failure with complete
evidence is counted, while incomplete observation/reconciliation is INCOMPLETE.

**Aggregate measurement acceptance controls:** for each spike, retain a full
positive result with the right mode, all applicable exact fields, and source
records sufficient to recompute every declared value. A single-field negative
must reach that field's intended guard. Where the protocol specifies
`INCOMPLETE` (undefined envelope, absent tuple inventory, missing required run
or result field), preserve that outcome distinctly from a valid `PASS`; whether
the package validator accepts the evidence as structurally readable is separate
from whether a gate accepts it. Keep DEV and QUALIFICATION fixtures distinct.

specific rule and gap in this section; **E** = only the branch evidence named
above, not per-path coverage; remaining mutations are **NOT RUN**.

I01–I25 batch, not a separate per-field release/test cycle. At plan revision

the path-description inventory only; aggregate field mutations remain NOT RUN.

### S2 aggregate latency source row — attempt 62 preregistration (plan 5.434)

The 18 frozen workload fields are the six `request_to_tx_commit_ms_{p50,p95,p99}` / `request_to_page_visible_ms_{p50,p95,p99}` values for each C-W0/C-W1/C-W2. Producer source is `S2Harness.executeWorkload`: successful capture rows store integer elapsed milliseconds; those exact observations feed the aggregate lists. `request_to_tx_commit_ms` is computed as `(tTxCommitNs-tIntakeNs)/1_000_000`, but the separate transaction timestamp is not serialized. `request_to_page_visible_ms` can be independently recomputed from successful-row `end_monotonic_ns-start_monotonic_ns`, since these endpoints are intake and page-visible time. Typed capture failures store `-1` sentinels and do not enter the lists. `S2Harness.percentile` uses nearest rank `ceil(p*n)-1`, returning 0 for empty lists. Host validation currently checks only aggregate numeric shape and does not recompute from retained successful rows.

Attempt 62 must source-bind all 18 aggregates and validate every source's typed domain and success/failure eligibility. For an all-typed-failure workload, the frozen outcome rule allows this diagnostic-only failure without a kill; the producer's empty-list zero summary can be reconciled only when every required row is present and has both latency sentinels equal to -1. A missing row or invalid source remains an evidence failure. Exact DEV and QUAL matrix sizes are unchanged; QUAL remains 20×100 rows per workload. No latency SLA is added. Preregistered controls, source limits, evidence path and completion condition are in plan revisions 5.434–5.435.

### 11.20 S5 per-run source reconciliation — attempt 66 contract

**Scope/source.** This is a child slice of `ENG-B1B2-INVENTORY-01`, under I14–I20, not a release package. Normative behavior remains Frozen Spike Protocol v1.1 §§7.5–7.10. It covers the existing DEV scenarios `SUCCESS`, `KNOWN_FAILURE`, `E1`, and `E2`; provider tuple inventory and QUAL remain incomplete because INPUT-05 is unresolved.

**Source gap (confirmed by code inspection at attempt-66 baseline).** `S5Pipeline.evaluateObservation` queries provider display name/size and reads bytes to derive booleans, but discards the observed byte count and SHA-256. `S5Harness` serializes fixture identity/size/hash and aggregate counters, but its per-run log omits fixture, operation, provider read-back, measurement provenance, error category and source process identity. `host/runner.py` checks transaction depth, write count, state, death, and SUCCESS booleans, but does not reconcile those source fields to fixture/log/metrics. KNOWN_FAILURE rotates three scenario strings but the provider exception evidence is swallowed by the pipeline; quota simulation uses a concrete IOException path, so no new exception class is assumed.

**Finite matrix / mutations.** From a valid S5 DEV package, test all four scenarios and each relevant field independently: missing/null/wrong-type/negative `fixture.size_bytes`; malformed or mismatching `fixture.sha256`; foreign/missing fixture `artifact_id`; missing or malformed `provider_bytes_observed`; wrong/missing source size/hash or disagreement among fixture, provider observation and metric; missing/wrong `measurement_source`, execution PID or PID relation to the harness PID; non-finite/negative/wrong-unit duration; stale or malformed per-run log, and run/operation/scenario/state/PID/fixture/provider mismatch between log and JSON; invalid scenario-specific observation shape; KNOWN_FAILURE category mismatch and absent concrete exception class/error code evidence; E1/E2 revalidation fields inconsistent with read-only status, zero writes, death, terminal state, or available provider evidence. Keep E1's before-call absence distinct from E2's post-call unknown outcome. Positive controls cover successful full read-back, each frozen known-failure setup label, E1 and E2. Preserve DEV outcome and INPUT-05 qualification INCOMPLETE semantics. No payload contents enter diagnostic logs.

**Implementation boundary.** Add source values already measured by the current S5 producer to structured per-run evidence and bind them in the host validator; retain exact existing scenario count, frozen thresholds, terminal-state behavior, write policy and provider qualification status. Do not call mock-provider evidence physical or production-provider qualification. If a measured source cannot be faithfully captured, record that limitation rather than synthesizing a value.

**Acceptance.** Committed source and collected regression tests accept the complete valid four-scenario synthetic DEV fixture, reject each isolated missing/type/domain/cross-source/log mutation at its intended S5 guard, and keep aggregate counters/outcome and `qualification_outcome=INCOMPLETE` consistent. ### 11.22 S6 startup-barrier availability consistency — attempt 68 contract

**Scope/source.** Child slice of `ENG-B1B2-INVENTORY-01` / I12 + I20, following attempt 67. Normative fields are Protocol §8.6–8.8 `startup_barrier_ms_p50/p95/p99` and `per_hook_result_table_location`. Current S6 producer deliberately writes null quantiles and `startup_barrier_measurements_available=false` because it kills the isolated `:pipeline` process and does not observe an application startup barrier. Do not substitute process-death latency, synthesize zero, or change this evidence limitation.

**Confirmed gap.** `aggregate_metric_errors` validates the percentiles as numbers when outcome PASS and `s6_per_hook_table_errors` validates the availability flag's Boolean shape and per-hook run totals. They do not cross-check the table availability flag against percentile nullability. Thus an INCOMPLETE record can claim unavailable measurements while supplying numbers, or say measurements are available while all percentiles are null, without the source-consistency guard. `tests/test_aggregate_contract.py` covers each side separately but is not included in `tools/check_all.sh`'s explicit pytest target list.

**Finite matrix.** Positive: `startup_barrier_measurements_available=false` with all three aggregate values null and outcome INCOMPLETE or a truthful kill-driven FAIL; `available=true` with three finite nonnegative numeric values; PASS always requires numeric values and table. Negative from valid source pair: false + any numeric/zero value; true + missing/null/wrong-type/negative/nonfinite percentile; non-Boolean availability; false + PASS; missing/unsafe table path for PASS. Preserve valid kill-driven FAIL if frozen kill counter is positive and measurement availability is false. No performance threshold or source sample is invented.

**Acceptance.** Pure contract tests distinguish the valid unavailable/INCOMPLETE case from contradictory metrics; runner calls the helper after loading the same safely resolved per-hook table; tests assert intended diagnostics. Add `tests/test_aggregate_contract.py` to `tools/check_all.sh`'s pytest batch so the aggregate contract remains part of the configured candidate run. Record committed-source test output/checksum. No APK/Android runtime in this host-only slice; integrated `check_all.sh --rerun`, physical Xiaomi, independent review, owner acceptance and parent package remain open.

Pure contract tests cover valid unavailable and available states, contradictory/null/nonfinite values, malformed availability and PASS without measurements. Validator integration rejects both cross-source contradictions. ### 11.23 S2–S6 instrumentation completion-result consistency — attempt 69 contract

**Scope/source.** This is the I21 instrumentation-result class already inventoried in §§11.2, 11.14 and 11.19. The two producer/consumer routes are `_run_instrumented` for `run-suite` and the S2–S6 loop in `cmd_run_all`. Each names one specific JUnit method; source in `D1EncoderFinishedInstrumentedTest.java` confirms one `@Test` method per S2–S6 target. The optional `ALL` class target is separate and may report a larger positive count.

**Confirmed gap.** Both routes accept any stdout containing `OK (` and only reject `FAILURES!!!` or nonzero adb. This can accept `OK (0 tests)`, `OK (2 tests)`, explicit instrumentation abort/failure, or a failure code despite the named case not having completed as requested.

**Finite matrix.** Shared guard accepts rc=0 with exactly one `OK (1 test)` summary and no failure/abort marker or non-success final instrumentation code. For ALL-class mode, require exactly one positive-count `OK (N tests)` summary under the same failure/code checks. Reject zero, >1 for a named method, missing/duplicated/malformed summary, nonzero adb plus success-like text, `FAILURES!!!`, `INSTRUMENTATION_FAILED`, `INSTRUMENTATION_ABORTED`, and non-success final code. Test all five named methods through both routes; invalid results halt before evidence pulls/cycle validation/package success. Existing S1-specific command semantics remain unchanged.

This host slice does not claim Android execution or physical qualification; integrated full check/APK is reserved for the parent batch.

### 11.24 S1 resumed-run timing source path and required read-back — attempt 70 contract

**Scope/source.** Existing I23/I12 work in §§11.15 and 11.19. The user result is a supported S1 resume that replays missing barrier work, retains previous runs and recalculates an aggregate from evidence that can be independently source-checked. Protocol §9 requires raw log references and §3.8–3.9 defines S1 timings/counters. The 24-run DEV and 8×100 QUAL matrices are unchanged.

**Confirmed source mismatch.** In both normal and host-orchestrated S1 instrumentation branches, `S1Harness` writes the compact timing row to external `evidence/device_logs/S1/<run>.log` but assigns `raw_log_location=logs/S1/<run>.log`. The host S1 orchestrator writes its verbose run transcript at `logs/S1/<run>.log`; it pulls the matching device log separately and marks that pull optional. The S1 aggregate source validator reads `raw_log_location` and requires one compact identity/barrier/endpoint/metric row. Separately, the current resume integration fixture gives latency values to all barriers and generic text logs, then expects a PASS; this is false coverage and the test actually yields aggregate INCOMPLETE.

**Finite matrix.** Positive: complete 24-run DEV with null timing on D1–D4, publish timing on D5–D8, reference timing only on D8, exact run/barrier/start/end/metric binding, and one retained source row per run; partial 23-run cycle plus missing D8 replay produces 24 source-valid rows and aggregate PASS while earlier evidence stays unchanged. Negatives: missing/empty/nonzero device timing-log read-back; foreign/duplicate run row; wrong barrier; endpoint or timing value disagreement; duplicate row; unsafe path. Each must prevent attempt SUCCESS and preserve prior cycle state/history. QUAL uses only its specified full matrix and is not simulated as physical evidence.

**Attempt 70 acceptance.** Point each S1 per-run raw-log reference to the actual retained device timing file; make its read-back mandatory for all S1 cases that reference it; preserve the verbose host transcript at its own path. Correct the end-to-end fixture to emit producer-shaped evidence and assert both resume status and aggregate result. Runner version increments from 1.23 only if source changes; harness `.1.21`/22 increments once for S1 producer change. Compile app/androidTest; connected API36.1 run if available, otherwise record NOT RUN. Preserve exact command/output/evidence and parent final check/APK boundary. No product behavior, S1 outcome rule, or acceptance threshold changes.

### 11.28 I19 health event process-name scope — attempt 74 contract (plan 5.463)

At baseline `9f3b8670`, `S3Harness.queryAnrOom` applies active-process UID/PID/package filters and historical package/UID/time filters. The host `health_event_source_errors`, however, accepts every nonempty `process_name`; an otherwise valid event from a foreign package passes its source schema. This leaves a host-verifiable I19 process-scope gap.

Use the current fixed `applicationId` `com.example.docscanspike`: accept the exact app process and Android child process names `com.example.docscanspike:<nonempty suffix>`; reject foreign package, false dotted prefix, empty child suffix, and missing/null/wrong-type/blank names. Apply to both S3 and S4, with positive exact-package and child-process controls. Do not claim process name proves UID; it complements the producer's UID/PID filter. This is host-only and does not change app/runner/product versions, frozen scope, thresholds or gate status. Evidence target `execution/device_tests/ENG-B1B2-INVENTORY-01/attempt-74-health-process-scope/`. Parent inventory and all runtime/physical/independent/owner acceptance remain open.

**Attempt 74 result (plan revision 5.464).** Host commit `eba0d167` constrains S3/S4 event `process_name` to the fixed app package or a colon-delimited Android child process. It rejects foreign packages, false dotted prefixes, empty child names and malformed values. The complete aggregate-contract suite passes 50/50 and S3/S4 integrated validators pass 2/2; Python compile, shell syntax and diff checks pass. Logs/checksums: `execution/device_tests/ENG-B1B2-INVENTORY-01/attempt-74-health-process-scope/`. This does not prove event UID independently or close runtime/physical I19 health qualification; parent inventory and MVP acceptance remain open.
